# The formal model

The core calculus has a trait's parameters, and their member types as elaboration declares them: an impl gives each of them a type that may name the impl's parameters, one impl per type constructor whatever its arguments (`TraitImpl.Implements`), a bound may give them types that name the function's type parameters, which a use's type argument meets through an impl or a bound with those arguments (`TraitArgs`, [traits.bound-arguments](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.bound-arguments)), and `Trait.m` at a type has the function's type with `Self` and the trait's parameters replaced by the type and the arguments that its impl or its bound gives. A bound that gives no arguments names the functions of a trait with parameters through member types, `C.Item`, which elaboration declares as type parameters of the function named `C.Item`, each the argument that the bound of `C` gives the trait's parameter `Item`, so the bound gives them all (`Decls.MemberTypes`, [traits.member-types](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.member-types)), and a `where` clause's bounds are those parameters' ([traits.where](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.where)).

`formal/` is a Lean 4 model of a core calculus of Stilt. It states the typing and mode rules of the `core` tier, as goal `sound-typing` asks ([Goals](../docs/design/goals.md)), with the views of [views.what-view-is](../reference/13-modes.md#views.what-view-is) as the reference interpreter holds them and as windows into their sources' storage, and the views that write of [projections.writable-views](../reference/13-modes.md#projections.writable-views) as calls that take their source `inout`, which write it at once in place and with windows ([Views in the calculus](#views-in-the-calculus)) and the effects and handlers of [handlers.with](../reference/12-effects.md#handlers.with), with the check of [handlers.resume-at-most-once](../reference/12-effects.md#handlers.resume-at-most-once) that a handler resumes at most once ([Effects and handlers in the calculus](#effects-and-handlers-in-the-calculus)), the projections of [projections.declaring-projection](../reference/13-modes.md#projections.declaring-projection) as forms of the calculus, with the checks of their rules and the promise that a projection's body runs one `yield` on every path ([Projections in the calculus](#projections-in-the-calculus)), the shared values, tasks, scopes and parallel calls of [shared.when](../reference/14-tasks.md#shared.when), [tasks.sequential-run](../reference/14-tasks.md#tasks.sequential-run) and [par.sequential-meaning](../reference/14-tasks.md#par.sequential-meaning) as the export writes them ([Shared values, tasks and parallel calls in the calculus](#shared-values-tasks-and-parallel-calls-in-the-calculus)), the cycles among shared values that [cycles.can-form-cycle](../reference/14-tasks.md#cycles.can-form-cycle) allows ([Accepted programs](#accepted-programs)), and, in calculi of their own, the views of [views.what-view-is](../reference/13-modes.md#views.what-view-is) as windows and the writable views of [projections.writable-views](../reference/13-modes.md#projections.writable-views), of the `advanced` tier ([Views](#views)), descending recursion under `without div` ([facts.descending-self-calls](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.descending-self-calls) and [facts.without-div](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.without-div)), the member types and kinds of [traits.member-types](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.member-types) and [traits.trait-parameters](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.trait-parameters) ([Trait parameters and kinds](#trait-parameters-and-kinds)), the rigid type parameters of [polymorphism.rigid-types](../reference/07-types.md#polymorphism.rigid-types) ([Rigid type parameters](#rigid-type-parameters)) and the fresh types of [meta.fresh-types](../reference/16-meta.md#meta.fresh-types), of the `meta` tier ([Fresh types](#fresh-types)), the record bounds of [records.record-bounds](../reference/07-types.md#records.record-bounds) ([Record bounds](#record-bounds)), the spreads over packs of [variadics.type-spreads](../reference/07-types.md#variadics.type-spreads) and [variadics.spread-expressions](../reference/07-types.md#variadics.spread-expressions) ([Spreads over packs](#spreads-over-packs)), the hygiene of the pattern macros of [macros.hygiene](../reference/16-meta.md#macros.hygiene) ([Hygienic expansion](#hygienic-expansion)), and the `when`s of [shared.when](../reference/14-tasks.md#shared.when) among threads and the folds of [par.fold-tree](../reference/14-tasks.md#par.fold-tree) ([Shared state and parallel folds](#shared-state-and-parallel-folds)). Every definition cites the paragraph of the Reference it models.

## Building

The model needs only Lean, at the version that [`lean-toolchain`](lean-toolchain) pins; [elan](https://github.com/leanprover/elan) installs it. It uses no library beyond Lean's own.

```sh
cd formal
lake build
```

A build from scratch takes about 40 seconds, and must finish with no errors and no warnings. No proof uses `sorry`, `admit` or an axiom of its own.

## Executable semantics

The model's evaluator also runs programs, as a third implementation of what they mean beside the reference interpreter and the compiler, which catches a misreading that both share. `stilt-ref model PROGRAM` writes a program without errors as the calculus writes it, in JSON ([`Stilt.Run.Decode`](Stilt/Run/Decode.lean) reads it): `main` and the functions it reaches, with the records and variants they name, a generic record or variant type such as `Option<T>` declared once, with its type parameters, and named at its arguments where it is used, but once for each list of arguments when it has value parameters or a pack, and a generic function, the prelude's list functions with code among them, once for each instantiation it is called at, its body at those types, and at the rows its non-escaping parameters' arguments bring ([effects.non-escaping-parameters](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.non-escaping-parameters)). A function that calls itself at ever deeper types ([polymorphism.recursion](../reference/07-types.md#polymorphism.recursion)) has no end of instances, and is declined. `stilt-model PROGRAM.json`, built by `lake build stilt-model`, runs it in the models of memory of copies and of in-place mutation, of windows when it makes a view ([Views in the calculus](#views-in-the-calculus)), and of sharing when it may share ([Accepted programs](#accepted-programs)), which must agree, and shows what the run shows as a process ([`Stilt.Run.Program`](Stilt/Run/Program.lean)): its standard output, its standard error, an escaping raise's `error: {e}` after it, and its exit status ([program.exit-statuses](../reference/04-programs.md#program.exit-statuses)). It doesn't check the program against `Program.Accepted`, which the model states as relations with no decision procedure, so these runs test the evaluators and their agreement with the other implementations, and `soundness` speaks of an exported program only when that program meets the theorem's hypothesis, which no tool checks of it yet.

Printing and reading are operations of the calculus on the world, which every model of memory carries beside its frames ([`Stilt.World`](Stilt/World.lean)): `print(s)` evaluates `s` and then appends it and a newline to the world's output, `eprint(s)` to its standard error, `io.write(s)` and `io.ewrite(s)` append `s` alone, and `io.flush()` does nothing, since it changes only when the output reaches stdout ([program.output-flushing](../reference/04-programs.md#program.output-flushing)). The world starts with the program's whole stdin as its input, each maximal invalid UTF-8 subsequence one U+FFFD; `io.read_line()` takes its first line, `Str.nextLine` and `Str.afterLine` ([std.read-line](../reference/17-std.md#std.read-line)), `io.poll_line()` reads as it does, since the whole input is waiting, and `io.read_all()` takes the rest ([std.io](../reference/17-std.md#std.io)). A call starts in its caller's world and its caller goes on in the world the call ends in, however it ends, so the run shows what was written before a raise that escapes `main` or a panic, from the world it ends in. Effects are declared once for each list of arguments they are used at ([Effects and handlers in the calculus](#effects-and-handlers-in-the-calculus)), and a performance's rest resumes in the world its handler's body leaves, so what a body writes stays written however the body ends. The cells of shared values are in the world too, so what a body does to them stays done once the rest resumes ([Shared values, tasks and parallel calls in the calculus](#shared-values-tasks-and-parallel-calls-in-the-calculus)). A few forms the export writes in the calculus's own terms: a constant's value, a constant expression ([declarations.constant-expressions](../reference/10-declarations.md#declarations.constant-expressions)), stands where it is read, and a constant that a pattern names is declared with its type and value, the pattern written as the constant and the literal pattern of its value; `let` and `var` with a pattern are a `match` with one arm; `xs.get(i)` is an `if` on `0 <= i < len` ([std.lists](../reference/17-std.md#std.lists)); and a store that also reads its place, `p op= e` or a call with an `inout self` receiver, binds the place's indexes once, then the other operands, then reads it ([values.compound-assignment-order](../reference/05-values.md#values.compound-assignment-order) and [values.inout-argument-order](../reference/05-values.md#values.inout-argument-order)).

The export writes some forms in the calculus's terms: a generic record or variant type as one declaration for each list of arguments it is used at, an anonymous record type as a record named by its own text, `{ f: T, … }`, its fields in the alphabetical order of their names, which shows without a name and has only the structural impls ([records.field-order](../reference/07-types.md#records.field-order), [records.no-home](../reference/07-types.md#records.no-home)), a value parameter read in a body as the literal its instance gives ([generics.value-parameters-in-body](../reference/07-types.md#generics.value-parameters-in-body)), `array.fill(x)` as the list of `n` copies and `array.from_list(xs)` as a test of its length ([generics.arrays](../reference/07-types.md#generics.arrays)), a writable binding of a view, an `inout` parameter of a view type and `view.split` as calls that take their source `inout` and the part's offsets ([Views in the calculus](#views-in-the-calculus)), a native that gives an `Option`, `find`, `rfind`, `str.from_utf8`, `str.from_code_point`, an integer type's `checked_add`, `checked_sub` and `checked_mul`, a narrowing `x.to_T()`, and a float's `x.to_T()` to an integer type, its truncation toward zero when it fits ([numbers.conversions](../reference/06-numbers.md#numbers.conversions)), as the calculus's list of none or one value, tested by its length; `s.to_f64()` and `s.to_f32()` as a test of `FloatVal.parse`'s grammar and then its value, rounded as a float literal is ([numbers.parsing](../reference/06-numbers.md#numbers.parsing)); and `strip_prefix` and `strip_suffix` as `starts_with` or `ends_with` and a `slice`, `split_once` and `rsplit_once` as `find` or `rfind` and two slices, `cli.args()` as `[]`, since the runner gives the model no arguments ([std.cli](../reference/17-std.md#std.cli)), `x.show()`, `x.show_nested()` and `x.eq(y)` through a structural impl as a splice, the text of the list of `x` without its brackets, and `==` ([traits.structural-calls](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.structural-calls)), an array as the list of its elements, which no value, index, order or text of a run tells apart ([generics.array-elements](../reference/07-types.md#generics.array-elements), [generics.array-traits](../reference/07-types.md#generics.array-traits)), a `for` over a map as one over its entries ([expressions.for-iterables](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.for-iterables)), `xs.sorted_by(key)` and the other sorts of std.lists as `key` called once for each element, in order, its keys collected, then a stable merge sort by them ([std.lists](../reference/17-std.md#std.lists)), `x.compare(y)` at a type whose `Ord` is structural as the `Ordering` its structural order gives ([traits.structural-calls](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.structural-calls)), a trait's default method or function without `self`, at a type whose impl leaves it out, as a function of its own with `Self` that type and the trait's parameters the impl's arguments ([traits.impl-members](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.impl-members)), a trait's function named on a type, `T.empty()` or `i64.empty()`, as the function of the impl for that type, at the arguments that the type gives the impl's parameters, those that the member types of the impl's bounds imply among them ([traits.functions-without-self](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.functions-without-self), [traits.member-types](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.member-types)), a trait constant read at a type, `T.ZERO`, as the expression of its value, the impl's or the trait's default, at that type ([traits.trait-constants](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.trait-constants)), a record update `{ ..base, f: e }` as `base` read, the fields written, then the record built ([records.update](../reference/07-types.md#records.update)), a constant in a pattern whose value is no literal of an integer type, `bool`, `str`, `()` or a tuple of those as a fresh name in its place compared with `==` to the constant in the arm's guard ([patterns.constants](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.constants)), `m[k].f = v` as a read of `m[k]` and an `insert`, `s.to_i64()` as a test and a read, `xs.pop()` as a read of the last element and a `slice` stored, `f(inout m[k])` as `m[k]` read into a fresh variable when the call starts, which the call takes `inout`, and stored back with `m[k] = v` when it returns or raises ([std.finding-keys](../reference/17-std.md#std.finding-keys), [values.inout-argument-order](../reference/05-values.md#values.inout-argument-order)), a `Lazy<T>` as its function, `fn() -> T` with an empty row, which `get` calls, since no program can tell when it runs or how often ([polymorphism.lazy-unobservable](../reference/07-types.md#polymorphism.lazy-unobservable)), `==`, `<`, a splice, a sort and a structural impl's `hash` at a tuple, a record, a variant type, a list or a map one of whose components has an impl of `Eq`, `Ord`, `Show` or `Hash` that the program writes as a call of the structural impl's function at that type, which the export declares once for each type it is used at: it calls each component's own impl from first to last, a record's fields in declaration order, stopping at the first unequal pair or the first `compare` that isn't `Equal`, compares variants first by their order and lists of different lengths as unequal without calling any, and finds each key of a map in the other ([traits.structural-calls](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.structural-calls), [traits.map-equality](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.map-equality)); a sort through a written `Ord` is a stable insertion sort, which calls `compare` in an order that [std.lists](../reference/17-std.md#std.lists) leaves unspecified, and the hash of a type whose impls are all structural is `0`, which equal values share ([traits.prelude-traits](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.prelude-traits)), a map whose keys have an `Eq` and a `Hash` that the program writes, at the key type or a part of it, as the list of its entries in insertion order, each with its key's hash, and each function that finds a key as a function of its own, which calls `hash` once, on the key, and then `eq` on each stored key of the same hash, in insertion order, until one is equal, the key the receiver ([std.finding-keys](../reference/17-std.md#std.finding-keys), [traits.hash-follows-written-eq](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.hash-follows-written-eq)): `m[k]`, `m[k] = v`, `insert`, `contains`, `get`, `get_or`, `remove`, `len`, `is_empty`, `keys`, `values`, `entries`, a `for` over it, `==` and its text, and no default, a unary operator on an operand that never gives a value as the operand alone, as `-return x` ([declarations.never](../reference/10-declarations.md#declarations.never)), `assert c` as a panic of the kind `assert` when `c` is false ([program.assert-expr](../reference/04-programs.md#program.assert-expr)), a constructor or a function without code of its own named as a value, such as `Some` or `str.len`, as the closure that calls it, which no run tells from it, and a call of a trait's method, or an operator or a splice through an impl of `Eq`, `Ord` or `Show` that the program writes, as a call of that impl's function, the one impl a type has of a trait ([traits.coherence](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.coherence)). A program without `main` runs nothing and ends with status 2 ([program.missing-main](../reference/04-programs.md#program.missing-main)). A program with errors is written as `run` runs it: code with an error as a panic of the kind `check-error` where it is reached, and its run ends with status 2 ([program.bodies-with-errors](../reference/04-programs.md#program.bodies-with-errors), [program.programs-with-errors](../reference/04-programs.md#program.programs-with-errors)); a test that checks its stderr, which holds the diagnostics, declines. Each of the two declines what it can't decide, ending with status 77, which no crash ends with: the export, a program that uses something the calculus lacks, which it names; the model, a run that panics where the test checks the whole of its stderr, the panic's message among it, which the calculus doesn't keep (a test's `panic-at`, the panic's place, isn't asked of the model: `just formal` runs it with `--locations absent`), a raise of a value whose `Show` it lacks, a run nested deeper than its fuel, and one that needs more processor time than the runner gives a test, since the model evaluates by rewriting values. A run that gets stuck, or whose models of memory disagree, ends with status 70. `just formal` builds the model and its executable, then runs every run test of the conformance suite through [`tools/runner/model.sh`](../tools/runner/model.sh): each program the model doesn't decline must give what its test expects, and the tests it declines must be those that [`tools/runner/model-declines.txt`](../tools/runner/model-declines.txt) lists, so that no test moves from passing to declined unseen, and a crash, which ends with another status, fails. It runs 655 of the 809 run tests and declines the others.

## The calculus

The calculus is the smallest one in which the questions of soundness can be stated, grown toward the `core` tier: functions, generic ones among them, whose type parameters may carry trait bounds, records, variants and tuples, every integer and float type with their operators, strings, maps and sets, methods with `self` and `inout self` receivers, traits and their impls, `let`, `var` and `inout`, `for`, `while` and `loop` with `break`, `continue` and `return`, `raise` and `try`, `match` over patterns and `if let`, effect rows, and effects with their operations, performances, handlers that resume or take control, `resume` and `with`.

It is elaborated. Every binder carries its type, every construct that needs an expected type carries it, every non-escaping parameter carries its implicit row variable, every use of a function carries the instantiation of its type parameters and of its row variables, implicit ones included, every call carries the type of its result, and every method call is the call of the method it finds, `Trait.m` at the receiver's type for a trait's method. The reference interpreter evaluates an elaborated program too, so the model needs no inference. A read of a variable at its last use may be marked `move x`, as the compiler infers it; typing has no rule for a mark, and the program it is checked as is the one with every mark read as the variable.

| Part | In the calculus |
| --- | --- |
| Types | the integer types `i8` to `u64`, `f32` and `f64`, `bool`, `str`, `()`, `Never`, records and variant types at as many type arguments as their declarations have type parameters, tuple types, `List<T>`, `Map<K, V>` and `Set<T>` whose keys have an integer type, `bool`, `str` or `()`, or are tuples or lists of such keys, or records or variant types at such keys whose fields or payloads are such keys, with no `Eq` or `Hash` that the program writes, function types with modes and rows, type parameters, `Self` among them, `View<T>` of `str` or a list, and `Shared<T>` of a type whose rows hold no row variable |
| Rows | `io`, at most one `raise<E>`, effects, the `resume` of the body of an operation of a handler that takes control, and row variables: those a function writes, and the implicit one of each non-escaping parameter; printing and reading need `io` in the row they sit in ([Code without `io`](#code-without-io)) |
| Expressions | variables and their last uses, literals of every numeric type, record and variant literals, tuples, list literals, field access, `.0` on a tuple, indexing, the arithmetic, wrapping, shift, bitwise and comparison operators on integers, the arithmetic and comparison operators on floats but `%`, the native methods of the float types, `sqrt`, `floor`, `ceil`, `trunc`, `abs`, `round`, `min`, `max` and `is_nan` ([std.floats](../reference/17-std.md#std.floats)), `!`, the conversions `.to_T()` that return `T` and `.wrap_to_T()`, a float's `.to_T()` to an integer type, string literals and interpolations of strings, integers, floats, Booleans and the other types that implement `Show` through the built-in and structural impls, `len`, `is_empty`, `slice`, `contains`, `starts_with` and `ends_with` on strings, `==` and the ordering operators on `bool` and `str`, and through the built-in and structural impls at the types that implement `Eq` or `Ord` (`Decls.Implements`): tuples, records, variants and lists, and, for `==`, maps and sets, which compare their entries in any order, `Map.new`, `Map.with_default`, `len`, `is_empty`, `contains`, `m[k]`, `get_or`, `insert`, `m[k] = v`, `remove`, `keys`, `values` and `entries` on maps, `Set.new`, `Set.from_list`, `len`, `is_empty`, `contains`, `insert`, `remove`, `to_list`, `union`, `intersection`, `difference`, `symmetric_difference`, `is_subset`, `is_superset`, `is_disjoint` and `==` on sets, `match` whose arms are patterns, `_`, names, literals of the integer types, `bool`, `str` and `()`, tuple, record and variant patterns with patterns nested in them, or-patterns and constants, each arm with a guard or none, and `if let`, let-else and the `Option` fallback `e₁ else e₂`, each kept beside the `match` it means (`Expr.form`) and typed by its own rule, a projection's body, its `yield p`, the value it ends with and a call of a projection where a place is, each kept beside the calls it means (`Expr.form`), calls with parameter modes, uses of generic functions, method calls, `Trait.m` at a type, which a method call through a bound or on a type with an impl calls, and which `==`, the ordering operators and an interpolation at a type whose `Eq`, `Ord` or `Show` the program writes call, closures, `let`, `var`, assignment to places, `if`, `for` over a list, `while`, `loop`, `break`, `continue` and `return`, `raise`, `try e` and `try e catch x => e`, `View.all` and a view's `copy`, the writable bindings that `view.into` and `view.part` give, places through them and `view.split` ([Views in the calculus](#views-in-the-calculus)), `Shared.new(v)`, `E.op(e)`, `with h { … }` and `resume(e)` |
| Declarations | records and variant types, generic ones among them, whose type parameters take no bounds ([declarations.type-parameters](../reference/10-declarations.md#declarations.type-parameters)), functions whose generics are type parameters, each with its bounds, and row variables, with parameters of function type non-escaping or `escaping`, `impl` blocks of records and variant types, generic ones among them, whose type parameters may carry bounds that a use of their functions meets ([traits.bounded-blocks](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.bounded-blocks)), whose functions are methods when their first parameter is `self` or `inout self`, and none of which has the name of a function of a trait that the type implements, through an impl that the program writes or a structural one ([traits.name-clashes](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.name-clashes)), traits whose functions are methods and functions without `self` ([traits.functions-without-self](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.functions-without-self)), with parameters of their own, which an impl gives arguments and a bound may give them ([Trait parameters and kinds](#trait-parameters-and-kinds)), impls of a trait for a numeric type, `bool`, `str`, `()`, a record or a variant type, a generic one at the impl's type parameters, whose bounds a type's arguments meet for the impl to apply to it ([traits.implementing-trait](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.implementing-trait)), effects with their operations, and constants, whose values are constant expressions |
| Programs | the declarations, and `main`, which takes no parameters, returns `()`, and whose row holds no effect |

Left out: value parameters and packs of records and variant types, impls for `List<T>`, tuples and function types, bounds of an impl's parameters that give a trait arguments, generic methods, defaults but those of a trait's functions, which the export writes as the members of the impls that leave them out, a trait's constants, which the export writes as their values, the built-in and structural impls of `Eq`, `Ord` and `Show` as impls that `Trait.m` names, which are the calculus's operations, `where` clauses but as the bounds of member types, `where` clauses of trait impls, and record bounds, error types that name a type parameter, type parameters in rows, value parameters and packs, inference, `%` on floats, which is `fmod`, the conversions that return an `Option` and parsing, which need `Option<T>`, bounds of `Eq`, `Ord` and `Show` met through impls that the program writes, and structural impls that reach such an impl through a component, map keys of the other types that implement `Eq` and `Hash`, those whose `Eq` and `Hash` the program writes among them, and of type parameters, the other functions of maps and sets, among them `get` and the value that `remove` gives, which are `Option`s, and those that take a closure, places through a map element, as `m[k].f = v` and an `inout m[k]` are, the `Hash` of sets, the other natives of `str`, list patterns and patterns in `let` and `for` heads, which another calculus states ([Guards and list patterns](#guards-and-list-patterns)), patterns in `var` and closure parameters, `var` bindings in patterns, the exhaustiveness check's completeness, its witnesses and unreachable arms, key paths and field reads through options, the rest of the standard library, modules, implicit effects, and warnings. Of effects and handlers, it leaves out an effect's type parameters, which the export instantiates, an operation's own row, handler types, whose handlers are records of closures, and the check of [handlers.with-pairing](../reference/12-effects.md#handlers.with-pairing), which type soundness doesn't need. Indexing out of bounds, integer overflow, integer division by zero, slicing a string out of bounds or inside a UTF-8 sequence, and indexing a map without a default with a missing key are the operations that panic. Printing and reading are operations on the world ([Executable semantics](#executable-semantics)), natives whose rows hold `io`: each needs `io` in the row of the code it sits in, or it is E0502, as a call of a function whose row holds `io` does ([effects.missing-effects](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.missing-effects), `HasType.op`). Views are in the calculus as the reference interpreter holds them, a view as the part it reads, and views that write as calls that take their source `inout` ([Views in the calculus](#views-in-the-calculus)); views as windows into their sources' storage are a model of memory of their own, which runs the calculus's programs, and `soundness` proves its agreement with copies under [views.escaping](../reference/13-modes.md#views.escaping) and [views.change-while-viewed](../reference/13-modes.md#views.change-while-viewed) ([Views in the calculus](#views-in-the-calculus)). Calculi of their own, which [Views](#views) describes, prove the same of views of lists and of the writable views of [projections.writable-views](../reference/13-modes.md#projections.writable-views) held as windows written through at once.

An integer is the same value at every integer type that holds it, and an integer literal has every integer type that holds it, as the context it is elaborated in chooses ([numbers.integer-literals](../reference/06-numbers.md#numbers.integer-literals)). So an integer operation computes with integers and checks that its type holds the result: `+`, `-`, `*`, unary `-` and `/` panic with the kind `overflow` when it doesn't, `/` and `%` panic with the kind `divide-by-zero` on a zero divisor, and `MIN % -1` overflows ([numbers.integer-panics](../reference/06-numbers.md#numbers.integer-panics)). The wrapping operators, the shifts and the bitwise operators compute on the value's bits in two's complement, as Lean's `BitVec` does ([numbers.wrapping](../reference/06-numbers.md#numbers.wrapping), [numbers.shifts](../reference/06-numbers.md#numbers.shifts) and [numbers.bitwise-operators](../reference/06-numbers.md#numbers.bitwise-operators)). Floats are Lean's `Float32` and `Float`, IEEE 754 binary32 and binary64 rounded to nearest with ties to even ([numbers.ieee-754](../reference/06-numbers.md#numbers.ieee-754)), so their operations hold as Lean's do, and no proof by evaluation can run one. A float prints in the shortest form that reads back as it ([numbers.printing-floats](../reference/06-numbers.md#numbers.printing-floats)), whose digits `Stilt.FloatText` computes exactly from the float's bits ([numbers.float-digits](../reference/06-numbers.md#numbers.float-digits) and [numbers.float-notation](../reference/06-numbers.md#numbers.float-notation)). `&&` and `||` are `if`s, since they evaluate their right operand only when the left one doesn't decide the result ([values.left-to-right](../reference/05-values.md#values.left-to-right)).

A string is Lean's `String`, a sequence of Unicode scalar values, whose length and offsets count the bytes of its UTF-8 encoding ([std.bytes-and-scalars](../reference/17-std.md#std.bytes-and-scalars) and [std.str](../reference/17-std.md#std.str)). An interpolating literal is the operation that joins its pieces, each interpolation shown by the native `Show` of its type ([lexical.interpolation](../reference/02-lexical.md#lexical.interpolation) and [std.show-output](../reference/17-std.md#std.show-output)), and `==` and `<` on `bool` and `str` are their native `Eq` and `Ord` ([traits.operators](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.operators) and [traits.built-in-impls](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.built-in-impls)).

A tuple is a record whose fields are named by their positions, `0`, `1`, …, and whose type no declaration names ([declarations.tuples-and-unit](../reference/10-declarations.md#declarations.tuples-and-unit)). So `e.0` reads a field ([types.field-access](../reference/07-types.md#types.field-access)), a place such as `t.0` or `xs[i].1` has the field in its shape, and `t.0` and `t.1` don't overlap ([modes.places](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.places) and [modes.overlap](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.overlap)). A tuple literal is one of the calculus's built-in operations (`Op`), each of which evaluates its operands from left to right ([values.left-to-right](../reference/05-values.md#values.left-to-right)) and computes its value from theirs.

A `match` tries its arms in order and runs the first whose pattern matches ([patterns.match](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.match)), with the parts of the value its pattern binds bound to the names it binds, each a copy ([patterns.bindings-are-copies](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.bindings-are-copies)); `if let p = e { a } else { b }` is `match e { p => a, _ => b }` ([expressions.if-let](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.if-let)). A pattern is checked against the scrutinee's type and gives the types of the names it binds, which are distinct and immutable ([patterns.pattern-types](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.pattern-types) and [patterns.var-bindings](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.var-bindings)); an integer literal fits an integer type that holds it, no literal fits a float ([patterns.floats](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.floats)), a record pattern lists some of its record's fields, each once, as one ending in `..` does, and an or-pattern's alternatives bind the same names at the same types ([patterns.or-patterns](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.or-patterns)). Elaborated, an arm carries the names its pattern binds, in the order they are written, as every binder carries its type. An arm's guard is a `bool` typed with those names in scope ([patterns.guards](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.guards)), tried with each way its pattern matches, the alternatives of an or-pattern from left to right and, of several, the leftmost one's varying slowest (`Pattern.ways`), until it is true; when it is false with every way, the next arm is tried. The guard and the body each run with the way's parts bound, which the guard, its names immutable, leaves as they were. A constant pattern `C` names a constant that the declarations list with its type and its value, a constant expression typed with the constants before it alone, so that none names another in a cycle ([declarations.constant-expressions](../reference/10-declarations.md#declarations.constant-expressions) and [declarations.constant-cycles](../reference/10-declarations.md#declarations.constant-cycles)); it carries the pattern of the constant's value, which `Decls.constPattern` computes, and matches as that pattern does, the values structurally equal to the constant ([patterns.constants](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.constants)). Exhaustiveness is a premise of the typing rule: a derivation of `Exhaustive` over the rows of the arms without a guard or a constant ([patterns.guards-and-constants](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.guards-and-constants)), whose rules are the steps of the usefulness algorithm of [patterns.algorithm](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.algorithm) over the constructors of variant types, `bool`, `()`, tuples, records and lists by their lengths, a type without values needing no row ([patterns.types-without-values](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.types-without-values)), so that a constructor whose payload has none needs none either, and every other type covered only by patterns that match anything. The elaborator discharges it, as the checker's algorithm does; `Exhaustive.sound` proves that every derivation covers every value, so a well-typed `match` always finds an arm, one without a guard if no earlier one's guard is true. `exhaustive` (`Stilt.Exhaustive.Decide`) is the algorithm that finds a derivation, trying each type's step before the default one, and `exhaustive_sound` proves that what it accepts has one; it asks `Decls.uninhabited` (`Stilt.Inhabited`) whether a column's type has values, so a `match` on an `Option<Never>` is exhaustive with `None` alone and one on `type Loop = | Loop(Loop)` with no arm. That it accepts every exhaustive `match` is unproved.

An interpolated value of a type other than an integer type, `bool` and `str` is shown as the prelude's structural impls of `Show` show it ([std.show-output](../reference/17-std.md#std.show-output) and [traits.show](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.show)): `Value.shown` writes lists, tuples, maps, sets, records and variants, names unqualified and strings nested quoted and escaped ([std.show-names-and-escapes](../reference/17-std.md#std.show-names-and-escapes)), and a value's text is its erased copy's (`Value.shown_erase`).

Of a list's own functions ([std.lists](../reference/17-std.md#std.lists)), the calculus has `len()`, `is_empty()` and the list that `push(x)` stores, the list with `x` after its last element, beside indexing, list literals and `for`, and a `for` over a range iterates the list of the integers it yields ([expressions.range-iteration](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.range-iteration)), its bounds evaluated once, before the first iteration; the others, `get` and `pop` among them, give an `Option` or a callback's results, which need the generic variant types the calculus lacks.

A map keeps its entries in insertion order, and its value is a record that no declaration names, of its entries and of its default, as a tuple's is a record of its elements ([std.maps](../reference/17-std.md#std.maps)); a set's is a record of its elements, the keys of the prelude's `Map<T, ()>` ([std.sets](../reference/17-std.md#std.sets)). So reading, writing and copying values, and both models of memory, treat their parts as those of a record and a list, and only the functions of maps and sets read them as entries. A function of a map or a set finds a key as [std.finding-keys](../reference/17-std.md#std.finding-keys) says, and its key type is an integer type, `bool`, `str` or `()`, whose `Eq` and `Hash` are built in ([traits.built-in-impls](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.built-in-impls) and [std.map-key-bounds](../reference/17-std.md#std.map-key-bounds)), or a tuple, a list, a record or a variant type of key types, whose structural impls compare it part by part ([traits.structural-impls](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.structural-impls)), never a float, a function type or a type with an `Eq` or `Hash` that the program writes (`Decls.Key`), so the keys of a map are distinct and which of them share a hash changes nothing. A call that changes its receiver, as `m.insert(k, v)`, `m[k] = v`, `m.remove(k)` and `s.insert(x)` do, is the assignment to the receiver's place of the value that its `inout self` writes back, and `m[k] += v` reads `m[k]`, then stores it. The functions of sets, which the prelude writes in Stilt, are operations of the calculus that give what [std.sets](../reference/17-std.md#std.sets) says, `==` among them, which finds the same elements in any order ([std.set-impls](../reference/17-std.md#std.set-impls)). The structural `==` compares a map, wherever it stands in a value, as [traits.map-equality](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.map-equality) says: maps of different lengths are unequal, and otherwise each key of the first, in insertion order, is found in the second with an equal value, whatever the order of the entries and the defaults; so `[a] == [b]` holds of maps of the same entries inserted in different orders, and `==` on two maps is what `MapOp.equal` gives with their values compared by `==` (`Value.same_mapOf`). It compares sets by their elements, in any order. `==`, the ordering operators and interpolation apply only at a type that implements `Eq`, `Ord` or `Show` (`Op.BoundsIn`, a premise of `HasType.op`): a type with a built-in impl, a tuple, list, record or variant type whose components implement it and for which the program writes no impl, a map that has `Eq` when its keys are key types and its values have `Eq`, and `Show` when both have `Show`, a set likewise, neither with `Ord`, and a type parameter whose bounds name the trait ([traits.built-in-impls](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.built-in-impls), [traits.structural-impls](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.structural-impls) and [traits.unsatisfied-bounds](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.unsatisfied-bounds)). Function types and `Shared<T>` implement none ([shared.std-sync](../reference/14-tasks.md#shared.std-sync)): the export tests whether two handles of a `when` name one cell with an operation of its own, `sameCell`. A bound `T: Eq`, `T: Ord` or `T: Show` is met by the same types, so an instance of a body that compares or shows values of `T` compares or shows them through these impls (`Decls.Implements.substTys`).

`while` and `loop` run turns of their body, and `break`, `continue` and `return` are jumps, each of type `Never` ([expressions.loops](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.loops), [expressions.break-and-continue](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.break-and-continue) and [declarations.never](../reference/10-declarations.md#declarations.never)). An evaluation ends with a value, a raise or a jump, each with the frame it leaves. A turn ends with its body's value or a `continue`, after which the loop goes on, or with a `break`, which ends the loop with `()`; a `for` loop takes its body's jumps as a `while` does. A call takes its callee's `return`, whose value is the call's, and the end of `main` takes `main`'s ([expressions.closure-boundaries](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.closure-boundaries)). Typing allows a `break` or a `continue` only inside a loop's body, which a `while`'s head is outside, types the body of each function and closure literal outside every loop, so no jump crosses a call, fits a `return`'s operand to its body's result type, and gives a `loop` type `()` when its body holds a `break` that exits it and `Never` otherwise ([expressions.loop-types](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.loop-types)). The effects of an expression carry that result type and whether a `break` and a `continue` may appear.

A generic function's body is checked once, with its type parameters rigid and their bounds all that it knows of them ([traits.unsatisfied-bounds](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.unsatisfied-bounds); [polymorphism.rigid-types](../reference/07-types.md#polymorphism.rigid-types)): `x.m()` on `x: T` is the call `Trait.m(x)` at `T`, which a bound `T: Trait` allows. A use gives each type parameter a closed type that names none, which meets the parameter's bounds where the use is ([types.no-type-arguments](../reference/07-types.md#types.no-type-arguments)), and a call runs the body with its type parameters instantiated, as it does its row variables. `Trait.m` at a type with a head runs the function of the impl for that head ([traits.qualified-calls](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.qualified-calls) and [traits.coherence](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.coherence)), whose signature is the trait's with `Self` the head ([traits.impl-signatures](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.impl-signatures)), and in an instance every type is ground, so a bound that typing allowed is met by an impl. `HasType.substTys` proves that instantiating a body's type parameters with such types keeps its typing, so the fundamental lemma covers every instance of every body. Type parameters stand outside rows: a row's error type names none, so an instantiation of type parameters never meets a row.

`main` is checked as `fn main() / row` ([program.main](../reference/04-programs.md#program.main)): it returns `()`, which a `return` in it gives too, and its row holds no row variable, so it is a subset of `{io, raise<E>}`. [program.main](../reference/04-programs.md#program.main) also asks for `E: Show`, a trait of the prelude, which the model leaves out. So a run shows a value that `main` raises as the value itself, where the Reference prints the text its `Show` gives ([program.exit-statuses](../reference/04-programs.md#program.exit-statuses)). Most of the examples show a value by raising it from `main`.

## Modules

Each module of the model, with the first sentence of its doc and the paragraphs of the Reference that its declarations cite. `just proof-map` writes this table and the next ones from the doc comments of the compiled model, and `just formal` fails when they differ from what it writes; the site's proof map shows every declaration.

| [`Stilt.Alloc.Calls`](Stilt/Alloc/Calls.lean) | The steps of a run that read and write the frame, evaluate the parts of an expression in order, and call a function value, kept by `Eval.Counts`: the counting model reads, writes, binds and calls as copy-in, copy-out does, with the count beside its frame, and a call of a function value that allocates nothing when called runs a body that makes no value needing storage, a closure's own or that of a function the set covers (`Decls.MakesCover`), its type parameters and row variables replaced (`Stilt.Alloc.Instances`). |  |
| [`Stilt.Alloc.Count`](Stilt/Alloc/Count.lean) | The reference interpreter's evaluator with a count of the allocations it makes beside its state: `Count.eval` is `eval` in copy-in, copy-out, step for step, but that each operation that makes a value needing storage of its own adds one to the count when it makes it (facts.allocation and facts.native-allocation): a list literal, a closure literal, a record literal of a recursive type, a variant's constructor with a payload of one, a native that allocates, a `with` and a performance. | [facts.allocation](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.allocation) |
| [`Stilt.Alloc.Instances`](Stilt/Alloc/Instances.lean) | A call runs its callee's body instantiated, its type parameters and its row variables replaced (types.no-type-arguments and effects.row-variables). |  |
| [`Stilt.Alloc.Limits`](Stilt/Alloc/Limits.lean) | The checks of `without alloc` and `without copy`, as the Reference infers facts (facts.inferred-facts and facts.inference): a function has a fact when its own body holds a site of it, or names a function that has it, the functions of a recursive group solved together. | [facts.inference](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.inference), [facts.allocation](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.allocation), [facts.native-allocation](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.native-allocation), [facts.writes-in-place](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.writes-in-place), [facts.copy-at-call](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.copy-at-call), [modes.shared-writes](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.shared-writes), [facts.copy](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.copy), [facts.without-alloc](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.without-alloc), [facts.without](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.without), [facts.own-storage](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.own-storage), [facts.calls-through-function-values](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.calls-through-function-values), [facts.without-copy](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.without-copy) |
| [`Stilt.Alloc.Runs`](Stilt/Alloc/Runs.lean) | A `match` runs its arm with the parts its pattern binds, each of which allocates nothing when called when the value doesn't; a `for` loop runs its body with each element; a `when` takes values out of its cells and puts them back, and its cells keep values that allocate nothing when called (shared.when). |  |
| [`Stilt.Alloc.Sites`](Stilt/Alloc/Sites.lean) | The operations that add the implicit effect `alloc` by making a value that needs storage of its own (facts.allocation): a list literal, a closure literal, a string literal that interpolates and the pieces it shows, a record literal of a recursive type and the constructor of a variant with a payload of one, a `with`, a performance of an operation, and a native function that allocates, which is every native but those facts.native-allocation names (`Op.allocates`). | [facts.native-allocation](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.native-allocation), [facts.div](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.div), [views.what-view-is](../reference/13-modes.md#views.what-view-is), [facts.allocation](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.allocation), [declarations.recursive-types](../reference/10-declarations.md#declarations.recursive-types), [facts.inference](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.inference), [facts.calls-through-function-values](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.calls-through-function-values) |
| [`Stilt.Alloc.Steps`](Stilt/Alloc/Steps.lean) | `Eval.Counts P m' m`: run from a state whose values allocate nothing when called, with any count `c` beside it, the counting step `m'` ends as the step `m` of copy-in, copy-out does, with `c` beside each of its states, so it counts no allocation; and `m` ends with a value that `P` holds of, a raise or a jump whose values allocate nothing when called, each in such a state, with a panic, out of fuel or stuck, never with a performance (`Outcome.Good`). | [facts.allocation](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.allocation) |
| [`Stilt.Alloc.Storage`](Stilt/Alloc/Storage.lean) | Where a binding holds its own storage (facts.own-storage), which writes through an index are in place (facts.writes-in-place), which parameters are in place (facts.in-place-parameters), and which arguments a call copies for an in-place parameter (facts.copy-at-call). | [facts.own-storage](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.own-storage), [facts.in-place-parameters](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.in-place-parameters), [facts.inference](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.inference), [facts.writes-in-place](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.writes-in-place), [modes.copy-on-write-types](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.copy-on-write-types), [facts.copy-at-call](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.copy-at-call), [facts.allocation](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.allocation), [modes.shared-writes](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.shared-writes) |
| [`Stilt.Alloc.Theorem`](Stilt/Alloc/Theorem.lean) | **Without alloc** (facts.without-alloc): code that holds no operation making a value that needs storage of its own, and names only functions whose bodies are such code (`Decls.MakesCover`), counts no allocation when it runs. | [facts.without-alloc](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.without-alloc), [facts.calls-through-function-values](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.calls-through-function-values) |
| [`Stilt.Alloc.Values`](Stilt/Alloc/Values.lean) | A value allocates nothing when called, under a set of functions `lim` covers, when every closure it holds has a body without an operation that makes a value needing storage (`Expr.allocFreeIn`), and every function it names is one `lim` covers, so that calling it runs such a body, wherever the value was made (facts.calls-through-function-values). | [shared.when](../reference/14-tasks.md#shared.when) |
| [`Stilt.Cells`](Stilt/Cells.lean) | Where the cells of a world come from as `when`s hold them and put values back, and what is true of every value a world keeps: holding a cell, putting a value back, letting cells go and making a cell keep a property of every cell's value that the values put in have (`World.All`). |  |
| [`Stilt.Counterexamples.Aliasing`](Stilt/Counterexamples/Aliasing.lean) | A program that shows that `InPlaceAgreement` needs exclusivity: a well-typed call with two overlapping `inout` arguments, which modes.overlapping-inout rejects, tells copy-in, copy-out from in-place mutation. | [modes.overlapping-inout](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.overlapping-inout) |
| [`Stilt.Counterexamples.Checks`](Stilt/Counterexamples/Checks.lean) | Lemmas that turn the side conditions of typing into Boolean checks, which `decide` evaluates on the closed programs of the counterexamples, and what typing knows of a function named as a value. | [effects.passing-to-paired-variables](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.passing-to-paired-variables), [effects.paired-row-variables](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.paired-row-variables) |
| [`Stilt.Counterexamples.Ends`](Stilt/Counterexamples/Ends.lean) | `Decls.Ranked.call` fails without the check of facts.descending-self-calls and facts.shrinking-recursion: `fn f(n: i64) -> i64 { f(n) }` passes its parameter whole, neither a strict part of it nor a counter stepped toward a bound, so no ranking and no way of going down accepts it (`unranked`), and a call of it runs out of every fuel (`call_diverges`). |  |
| [`Stilt.Counterexamples.Escape`](Stilt/Counterexamples/Escape.lean) | A program that shows what the no-escape rule of effects.no-escape is for. |  |
| [`Stilt.Counterexamples.Packs`](Stilt/Counterexamples/Packs.lean) | `spread_map` holds at every pack, the empty one included, because a spread only maps over a pack tuple. |  |
| [`Stilt.Counterexamples.Paired`](Stilt/Counterexamples/Paired.lean) | Programs that show what effects.paired-row-variables is for. | [effects.no-escape](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.no-escape), [effects.catch-around-row-variables](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.catch-around-row-variables), [effects.paired-row-variables](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.paired-row-variables), [effects.non-escaping-parameters](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.non-escaping-parameters) |
| [`Stilt.Counterexamples.PassedOn`](Stilt/Counterexamples/PassedOn.lean) | Programs that show what effects.passing-to-paired-variables is for. | [effects.catch-around-row-variables](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.catch-around-row-variables), [effects.paired-row-variables](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.paired-row-variables), [effects.one-error-type](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.one-error-type), [effects.passing-to-paired-variables](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.passing-to-paired-variables) |

| [`Stilt.Else`](Stilt/Else.lean) | A let-else and an `Option` fallback run as the `match` each means, one step of fuel later, in every model of memory: `eval_form`. |  |
| [`Stilt.Ends.Body`](Stilt/Ends/Body.lean) | Code free of `div` halts from every state whose frame meets its roles, given that the calls it may make halt (`CallsHalt`): of a function ranked below the one whose body it is, and of that function itself on arguments whose measure is smaller than the call's the body runs, each on arguments that pass function values that halt where the function's body calls through its parameters, and through a non-escaping parameter, whose value the call vouches halts (`eval_halts`). | [facts.div](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.div) |
| [`Stilt.Ends.CountInstances`](Stilt/Ends/CountInstances.lean) | A call instantiates the type parameters and the row variables of the body it runs. |  |
| [`Stilt.Ends.Counter`](Stilt/Ends/Counter.lean) | In copy-in, copy-out: a bound that ends with an integer has the value `Bound.val` gives, which only the variables it reads decide (`eval_bound`, `Bound.val_congr`); a condition that counts and holds compares the counter's integer with the bound's (`eval_count`); and a body that steps the counter and ends, or continues, has moved it toward the bound (`eval_steps`). |  |
| [`Stilt.Ends.Counting`](Stilt/Ends/Counting.lean) | A `while` ends, and adds no `div`, when it counts (facts.counting-loops): its condition compares a variable, the loop's **counter**, with a **bound**, by `<`, `<=`, `>` or `>=`, alone or as the first conjunct of an `&&` chain; the bound is made of literals, variables and lengths of lists, by `+`, `-` and `*`; the body steps the counter toward the bound by a positive literal, `v = v + k` or `v += k`, as a statement of its own block, with no `continue` before it; and neither the body nor the condition writes the counter elsewhere or anything the bound reads. | [facts.counting-loops](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.counting-loops) |
| [`Stilt.Ends.Depths`](Stilt/Ends/Depths.lean) | Instantiation rewrites types alone, so an instantiated body has the same depth as the body. |  |
| [`Stilt.Ends.Descent`](Stilt/Ends/Descent.lean) | What the roles promise of a run, in copy-in, copy-out, whatever its order of write-back. |  |
| [`Stilt.Ends.Entry`](Stilt/Ends/Entry.lean) | A call starts with its descending parameter, its counter and its bound holding what its arguments passed (`enter_lookup`), so its body's frame meets the roles the body starts with, against what the body knows of the call (`enter_roles`). |  |
| [`Stilt.Ends.Halts`](Stilt/Ends/Halts.lean) | A loop whose number of turns its values decide needs more fuel the more turns it makes, so no bound that the text gives covers every run. | [shared.when](../reference/14-tasks.md#shared.when) |
| [`Stilt.Ends.Instances`](Stilt/Ends/Instances.lean) | Instantiation rewrites types alone, so an instantiated body holds the same sites of `div`, gives its variables the same roles, and has the same depth, as the body. |  |
| [`Stilt.Ends.NoCont`](Stilt/Ends/NoCont.lean) | Code free of `continue` outside its own loops and closure literals never ends with a `continue` (`eval_noCont`), in any model of memory: a loop's turn takes in the `continue`s of its body, and a call those of the body it runs. |  |
| [`Stilt.Ends.Roles`](Stilt/Ends/Roles.lean) | The check of a function's self-calls follows what each variable of its body holds. | [facts.descending-self-calls](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.descending-self-calls) |
| [`Stilt.Ends.Runs`](Stilt/Ends/Runs.lean) | The evaluator's steps that run expressions halt, in copy-in, copy-out, given that running an expression free of `div` whose depth is at most `k` does, from every state whose frame meets its roles (`HaltsRun`): lists of expressions, a record's fields, a place's indexes, a call's arguments, a `match`, an assignment and a `for` loop, whose turns cost no fuel. |  |
| [`Stilt.Ends.Start`](Stilt/Ends/Start.lean) | A call's frame meets the roles its body starts with (`enter_startRoles`): those of its self-calls (`FnDecl.entryRoles`), and its non-escaping parameters, which hold the function values that the call vouches halt (facts.div). |  |
| [`Stilt.Ends.Steps`](Stilt/Ends/Steps.lean) | `Eval.Ends P m`: from any state, `m` doesn't run out of fuel, and a value it ends with is one that `P` holds of. | [handlers.implicit-effects-and-capabilities](../reference/12-effects.md#handlers.implicit-effects-and-capabilities) |

| [`Stilt.Modes.Uses`](Stilt/Modes/Uses.lean) | Where a body uses a variable, read from its source: the occurrences of the name that refer to the binding, which an inner binding of the same name hides. | [modes.once-closures](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.once-closures), [modes.once-parameters](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.once-parameters), [patterns.guards](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.guards) |

| [`Stilt.Moves.Args`](Stilt/Moves/Args.lean) | Evaluating a call's arguments keeps the simulation, and more: each argument that is the last use of a variable passes the value that the variable still holds, live, when the call starts (values.passing-values and values.inout-argument-order). | [values.passing-values](../reference/05-values.md#values.passing-values) |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Back`](Stilt/Moves/Back.lean) | Copy-in, copy-out writes back only to the roots of `inout` arguments, which are live: so the write-back on the caller's frame and on its live bindings agree, and it keeps the frame's names. | [values.inout-arguments](../reference/05-values.md#values.inout-arguments) |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Call`](Stilt/Moves/Call.lean) | `invoke_step` runs a call of a body whose marks are last uses, from a caller `Ready` for it; `call_step` calls a function value, a closure or a declared function with its row variables instantiated (effects.inference). |  |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Cases`](Stilt/Moves/Cases.lean) | The two cases of the simulation where a last use hands storage over: a call, whose arguments may be last uses, and `let` or `var`, whose initializer may be one (values.passing-values). |  |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Control`](Stilt/Moves/Control.lean) | The steps of the evaluator that hold no last use of their own keep the simulation: a `catch`, the arms of a `match`, a loop, an assignment, a closure's capture, and the conversions of values. | [effects.catch](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.catch), [expressions.match](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.match), [expressions.for-iterables](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.for-iterables), [modes.change-while-iterating](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.change-while-iterating), [values.let-and-var](../reference/05-values.md#values.let-and-var), [values.assignment-order](../reference/05-values.md#values.assignment-order), [expressions.capture](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.capture) |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Drop`](Stilt/Moves/Drop.lean) | A call ends each variable it passes at its last use when it starts (values.passing-values). |  |
| [`Stilt.Moves.End`](Stilt/Moves/End.lean) | When a call ends, the callee has written below the places of its `inout` arguments, below the storage handed over to it, and in its own locations. |  |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Erase`](Stilt/Moves/Erase.lean) | `Expr.erase` turns every last use back into a read. |  |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Forms`](Stilt/Moves/Forms.lean) | One lemma for each form of expression that the simulation treats in more than a line: a call, `let` and `var`, indexing, assignment, `if`, the loops, `catch` and a closure literal. | [expressions.loops](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.loops), [expressions.break-and-continue](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.break-and-continue) |

| [`Stilt.Moves.Invoke`](Stilt/Moves/Invoke.lean) | A call keeps the simulation. |  |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Justified`](Stilt/Moves/Justified.lean) | A value is justified when every closure it holds has a body whose marks are last uses, so that calling it runs a body that `Moving` may run with its marks. | [values.passing-values](../reference/05-values.md#values.passing-values) |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Kills`](Stilt/Moves/Kills.lean) | * `Kills L X mk m ns`: `m` comes from `mk` by ending bindings whose names `X` holds and `L` doesn't, each the newest binding of its name, and revives none. |  |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Leave`](Stilt/Moves/Leave.lean) | Copy-in, copy-out's write-back after a call agrees with the store the callee left on every binding of the caller but those whose last use the call was (values.passing-values, values.inout-arguments and values.inout-argument-order), and the caller's state evolved only by writes below the places and the storage that the call passed. |  |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Maps`](Stilt/Moves/Maps.lean) | The functions of maps and sets see through marks: a key equals another as its erasure equals the other's, since keys are numbers, Booleans, strings and `()`, and every other part of a map or a set is carried as it is. | [traits.structural-calls](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.structural-calls) |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Marking`](Stilt/Moves/Marking.lean) | `Expr.markMoves` marks each argument and each initializer of `let` or `var` that is a variable at its last use, as the compiler infers it (values.passing-values). | [facts.last-use](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.last-use) |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Mask`](Stilt/Moves/Mask.lean) | A last use ends a variable while its binding is still in scope: nothing after it reads the variable, and under `Moving` its storage may belong to another variable. |  |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Matching`](Stilt/Moves/Matching.lean) | A pattern reads no closure's body, so it matches a value's erasure as it matches the value, and binds the erasures of the parts it binds; a `match` over erased arms selects the erasure of the arm it selects. |  |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Memory`](Stilt/Moves/Memory.lean) | A third model of memory, `Moving`: in-place mutation in which the last use of a variable hands its storage over instead of copying it (values.passing-values). | [values.passing-values](../reference/05-values.md#values.passing-values), [values.consumed-parameters](../reference/05-values.md#values.consumed-parameters) |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Oblivious`](Stilt/Moves/Oblivious.lean) | Copy-in, copy-out reads a mark as the variable: `eval` has one case for both, `Memory.bindInit` binds a copy, and `Copy.enter` binds a `moved` argument as a value (values.passing-values and values.inout-argument-order). | [values.passing-values](../reference/05-values.md#values.passing-values), [values.inout-argument-order](../reference/05-values.md#values.inout-argument-order), [values.inout-arguments](../reference/05-values.md#values.inout-arguments) |

| [`Stilt.Moves.Operations`](Stilt/Moves/Operations.lean) | Each operation of the evaluator keeps `MAgrees`: writing a live binding, binding a new variable, ending the newest binding, capturing the newest binding of each variable a closure literal captures when those are live, and binding the last use of a variable, which ends it and hands its storage over (values.passing-values). | [expressions.capture](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.capture), [values.passing-values](../reference/05-values.md#values.passing-values) |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Oracle`](Stilt/Moves/Oracle.lean) | Every expression, evaluated by copy-in, copy-out on a frame, ends as its erasure ends on the frame erased, up to the marks of the values it makes (`eval_erase`). | [values.passing-values](../reference/05-values.md#values.passing-values), [handlers.with](../reference/12-effects.md#handlers.with) |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Pass`](Stilt/Moves/Pass.lean) | `args` proves `ASim` for a call's arguments, evaluated from left to right (values.left-to-right). |  |

| [`Stilt.Moves.Runs`](Stilt/Moves/Runs.lean) | The combinators of `Stilt.Semantics` that evaluate subexpressions keep the simulation, given that evaluating each subexpression keeps it (`MRun`): the elements of a list, the fields of a record literal, the indexes of a place, and the arm of a `match`. | [expressions.match](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.match), [patterns.match](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.match), [patterns.guards](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.guards) |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Scopes`](Stilt/Moves/Scopes.lean) | The steps that bind a variable keep the simulation: an ordinary binding, in a new location, and the binding of a variable's last use, which ends that variable and hands its storage over (values.passing-values). | [values.passing-values](../reference/05-values.md#values.passing-values) |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Show`](Stilt/Moves/Show.lean) | A value's text is its erased copy's (values.passing-values): erasing changes only the bodies of closures, which no text shows. |  |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Simulation`](Stilt/Moves/Simulation.lean) | Evaluating a safe expression whose marks are last uses keeps the simulation of copy-in, copy-out by `Moving`, with any fuel: by induction on the fuel, case by case on the expression, as `InPlace.eval_agrees` is. |  |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Start`](Stilt/Moves/Start.lean) | Both models start a call from the live bindings of the caller alone: the places it reads are of live variables, and each last use passes the value its variable holds. |  |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Steps`](Stilt/Moves/Steps.lean) | The simulation of copy-in, copy-out by `Moving`, and the steps of the evaluator that keep it. | [expressions.break-and-continue](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.break-and-continue) |
| [`Stilt.Moves.Structural`](Stilt/Moves/Structural.lean) | Two values compare as their erased copies do (values.passing-values): erasing changes only the bodies of closures, which no comparison reads. |  |

| [`Stilt.Moves.Unmarked`](Stilt/Moves/Unmarked.lean) | A well-typed expression has no mark, since typing has no rule for one: its erasure is itself. |  |

| [`Stilt.OneShot.Calls`](Stilt/OneShot/Calls.lean) | A call of a one-shot function value, with one-shot arguments, performs what its body does: entering it binds one-shot values, its body passes the check, a closure's by the value and a declared function's by the program, and leaving it writes one-shot values back. |  |
| [`Stilt.OneShot.Handling`](Stilt/OneShot/Handling.lean) | The body of an operation of a handler that takes control runs the rest of the `with`'s block once for each `resume` it performs (handlers.resuming), so a body that performs at most one on each path runs it at most once, and the `with` performs no more `resume`s of the code around it than its block does: a `with` names an effect other than that of `resume`, so the block's pass through it. |  |
| [`Stilt.OneShot.Holding`](Stilt/OneShot/Holding.lean) | A `when` takes one-shot values out of a world whose cells hold one-shot values, and puts back the one-shot values of its frame; a `with` lets cells go without changing a value. | [shared.when](../reference/14-tasks.md#shared.when) |
| [`Stilt.OneShot.Instances`](Stilt/OneShot/Instances.lean) | Instantiation rewrites types and rows alone, and a row keeps its own `resume`, so an instantiated body performs as many `resume`s, and passes the check of handlers.resume-at-most-once as the body does. |  |
| [`Stilt.OneShot.Outcomes`](Stilt/OneShot/Outcomes.lean) | `Outcome.OneShot P n o`: on each path through `o`, at most `n` performances of `resume`, every value one-shot, a final value of which `P` holds, and every state one-shot: its frame and the values its world's cells hold. |  |
| [`Stilt.OneShot.Runs`](Stilt/OneShot/Runs.lean) | The evaluator's steps that run expressions keep `Eval.OneShot`, given that running checked code does (`OneShotRun`): a list of expressions, a record's fields, the indexes of a place, a call's arguments, a `match`, an assignment, a `for` loop, whose body, when it never resumes, may run any number of times, and a call, whose callee's body resumes at most once when a `with` that takes control calls it, and never otherwise. |  |
| [`Stilt.OneShot.Steps`](Stilt/OneShot/Steps.lean) | Each combinator of `Stilt.Semantics` keeps `Eval.OneShot` in copy-in, copy-out, whatever its order of write-back: a sequence of steps adds their counts, and a step that makes or moves values makes one-shot ones from one-shot ones. |  |
| [`Stilt.OneShot.Syntax`](Stilt/OneShot/Syntax.lean) | handlers.resume-at-most-once: a body resumes at most once on each path, and a `resume` is E0514 in a loop, in a closure literal, after another `resume` in sequence, or in a branch that runs after another that resumed. | [handlers.resume-at-most-once](../reference/12-effects.md#handlers.resume-at-most-once), [patterns.guards](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.guards), [handlers.resume-outside-operation](../reference/12-effects.md#handlers.resume-outside-operation) |
