The calculus of views that write (`Stilt.Writes`), in which a writable binding is a window written through at once, has lists only, whose places are a variable followed by indexes, so records, fields and their overlap are the views calculus's, and leaves out `view.part` and `view.split`, `inout` parameters of a view type, results that write, places through a view followed by further indexes, `View.of` of a temporary, loops and `if`. There, E0428 accepts a view that `view.into` made, as a block's last expression or not, which is every view that writes in that calculus. A view made from a writable binding has what the binding writes among its sources, so the model also rejects a block that hides one of those names and gives the view, which [views.escaping](../reference/13-modes.md#views.escaping) accepts.
- **A view that no binding holds** reads its sources while the expression that holds it still runs, which ends inside the statement that [views.reads-that-last](../reference/13-modes.md#views.reads-that-last) gives the read, so the model's law accepts every program that [views.reads-that-last](../reference/13-modes.md#views.reads-that-last) accepts, and some more.
- **[views.escaping](../reference/13-modes.md#views.escaping)** is checked where every scope ends, not only at a function's result. The model frees a binding's storage when its scope ends, and a window holds no reference of its own, as the compiler keeps a view's source alive by the reference that the source's binding holds (the rationale of [Modes](../reference/rationale/13-modes.md#views)). So the model also rejects a view that leaves an inner block whose binding it reads, as `let v = { var ys = [1, 2]; View.of(ys, 0, 1) }` does. It accepts a returned view of a temporary, which [views.escaping](../reference/13-modes.md#views.escaping) rejects: nothing can name or change a temporary, so a view of one is the part it reads.
- **Demands** ([modes.demands](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.demands)): the calculus checks the one demand it writes a site for, that the value `Shared.new` takes is `portable` (`Program.Demands`, E0432 of [shared.tasks-and-modes](../reference/14-tasks.md#shared.tasks-and-modes)). The export writes `task.spawn(f)`, a scope's `spawn` and the functions of `std.par` as calls and loops, so a task's function's demand (E0432) and the `calm` and `portable` demands of [par.what-runs-in-parallel](../reference/14-tasks.md#par.what-runs-in-parallel) (E0431) are left out, and no promise reads a demand or contention: the calculus runs one task at a time. A value captured from a `match` arm's binder of a value whose points the model can't tell counts as `fixed`, so the check may reject a program the checker accepts.
- **Last uses** ([facts.last-use](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.last-use)) are checked by bindings and in the order of evaluation (`Expr.movesOk`). By the order of evaluation, a use in a branch of an `if` or an arm of a `match` may be marked even when another branch uses the binding later in source order, since the other branch never runs after it: a strengthening, as the theorem proves those marks safe too. The order of evaluation also places the uses that other rules give and source order doesn't show: a call's `inout` arguments when it starts, after its other arguments ([modes.durations](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.durations)), a body's `inout` parameters when it ends, and a `when`'s names at the end of its block ([shared.when](../reference/14-tasks.md#shared.when)); so the mark in `f(inout a, move a)` is no last use. A guard marks no last use of what it reads: it may run again, with the next way its pattern matches ([patterns.guards](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.guards)), so what it reads stays live after it.
- **A constant in a pattern** carries the pattern of its value ([patterns.constants](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.constants)), which a value of a float type has none of, since no literal pattern fits a float ([patterns.floats](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.floats)): the model rejects a constant of a float type in a pattern. A value whose unary `-` overflows is rejected too, its pattern fitting no integer type, where the Reference panics at the constant's first use ([declarations.constant-evaluation](../reference/10-declarations.md#declarations.constant-evaluation)). The calculus has no `Eq` that a program writes, so [patterns.constants-with-written-eq](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.constants-with-written-eq) and its E0331 never apply. The export writes a constant as a constant pattern when its value is a literal of an integer type, `bool`, `str` or `()`, or a tuple of those, and any other as a fresh name compared with the constant by `==` in the arm's guard, which matches the same values when no `Eq` is written; a read of a constant is its value ([Executable semantics](#executable-semantics)).
- **Limits** are `without panic`, `without overflow`, `without div`, `without alloc` and `without copy` alone, joined from a function's clause and its module's header by the export (`Limits`), which writes none of them yet; the calculus has no `locks` limit, capabilities or `unsafe` ([facts.without](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.without), [facts.inferred-facts](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.inferred-facts)). The checks are stricter than the Reference's where the calculus can't see what the checker sees. A trait's method `t.m` counts as named for every impl of `t`, where the Reference names the one impl that the call's type picks ([facts.inference](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.inference)). `without overflow` is checked only beside `without panic`, and every operation that checks a range counts as a site of `overflow`, since the calculus's values carry no type ([Code without panics](#code-without-panics)): the proof of `without panic` follows untyped runs, in which `n / 2` overflows when `n` lies outside its type and `xs.len()` when a list is longer than an `i64` holds, so stating [effects.overflow-effect](../reference/12-effects.md#effects.overflow-effect)'s set, and `without overflow` alone, which rules out `overflow` and allows every other panic, would need that proof to follow the values' types and lists below the length of [facts.greatest-length](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.greatest-length). For `div`, a call through a bound, `Trait.m`, is a site in the calculus, though the export writes none: it writes generic code at each instance, and a call through a bound as a call of the impl's function, which adds no `div` when the function is ranked below its caller. An argument given to a non-escaping parameter is a site, but a closure literal, a function named that asks nothing of its arguments and a non-escaping parameter of the caller's, where [facts.div](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.div) counts any argument's sites where it is written; a call through a non-escaping parameter adds none. Self-calls that descend or shrink add none ([Descending recursion](#descending-recursion)), by rules narrower than [facts.descending-self-calls](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.descending-self-calls) and [facts.shrinking-recursion](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.shrinking-recursion): a step through an or-pattern or a list pattern, a `var` binding, and a self-call inside a closure literal, a loop, a `catch` handler or a `when` block don't descend; a self-call that shrinks steps by a positive literal toward a literal or a parameter, a literal constant being its literal in the calculus, and not by a name known positive, which is a range loop's variable, in whose loop no self-call descends; of the tests of [facts.guarding-tests](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.guarding-tests), only a comparison alone, mirrored or first in an `&&` chain guards its then-branch, and the negation of a comparison its else-branch; and a function's counter goes one way, toward one bound. The model also asks every self-call that shrinks to go toward one bound, where the Reference allows several bounds on the same side. A counting loop steps by a positive literal, `i = i + k` or `i += k`, not by a name known positive, which is a range loop's variable, whose loop's body the check of a `while` inside it doesn't follow ([facts.counting-loops](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.counting-loops)). The facts of the rules of acceptance are narrower too: no key that a store gives the code after it, no accumulator known non-negative, and a binding of a variable carries the variable's keys ([Code without panics](#code-without-panics)). Of [facts.what-proofs-change](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.what-proofs-change), the model states that a counting loop and self-calls that shrink add no `div`, and the operations the rules of acceptance prove no `panic` ([Code without panics](#code-without-panics)); the release builds that drop proved checks and the package API that the paragraph speaks of are outside the calculus. A call of `task.spawn(f)` or `s.spawn(f)` is a site of `div`, as [tasks.implicit-effects-and-capabilities](../reference/14-tasks.md#tasks.implicit-effects-and-capabilities) makes it: the export writes it as a call of `f` through a `let` binding that holds it, a call through a stored function value ([Shared values, tasks and parallel calls in the calculus](#shared-values-tasks-and-parallel-calls-in-the-calculus)), and a closure literal's body adds its sites where it is written.
- **A call through a function value**, a closure or a parameter of function type, consumes every argument when the model infers modes, since it doesn't know the callee's body: the conservative reading of "a parameter that its callee consumes" ([values.consumed-parameters](../reference/05-values.md#values.consumed-parameters); [modes.escaping](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.escaping) and [modes.copies](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.copies)). A call of a declared function reads the function's consumption. A call through a function value may store any argument when its result has a copy-on-write part or an argument is `inout`, as the call doesn't carry its parameters' types; an operation of the calculus, whose result type it doesn't carry either, may store each operand unless it is a length, a count, a test or a comparison; and what a call keeps goes to each of its `inout` arguments, whatever their types ([modes.call-stores](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.call-stores)). So the model may call shared some writes that the Reference calls unique.
- **[shared.names-that-may-be-one-place](../reference/14-tasks.md#shared.names-that-may-be-one-place)** is stated for the law of exclusivity, E0402 and E0404: the views' rules, E0423 and E0429, keep the names of a `when` apart. `Program.HeldNames.aliased` covers a block that binds none of its `when`'s names, as the export's blocks don't.
- **`without alloc` and `without copy`** ([facts.without-alloc](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.without-alloc) and [facts.without-copy](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.without-copy)) are checked by rules stricter than the Reference's in places: a modification is shared when the inference calls its binding shared at any of its modifications, where [modes.shared-writes](../reference/13-modes.md#modes.shared-writes) reads each modification; a parameter is in place when the body writes it through an index, passes it whole to an in-place parameter, or hands its storage on at a last use, whether or not that storage reaches a write in place ([facts.in-place-parameters](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.in-place-parameters)); an assignment inside a loop gives its binding storage of its own only on the turn that makes it ([facts.own-storage](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.own-storage)); a variant's payload whose type is a type parameter, in generic code, counts as one of a recursive type ([facts.allocation](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.allocation)); and every native of `Set` allocates, as [facts.native-allocation](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.native-allocation) names none, but `==`. The last uses are those of `Expr.markMoves`, by the order of evaluation (**Last uses** above). An `inout` argument that names a binding whole leaves it holding its own storage, as [facts.own-storage](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.own-storage) counts only assignments, though the callee may assign its parameter a value that another binding holds. A form counts as the code it means (`Expr.form`): a projection's body, its `yield p` and a call of a projection where a place is allocate in the model, since their meanings keep the values yielded in a list, push to it and pass the caller's access as a closure literal, where [facts.allocation](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.allocation) makes only a `yield` of a place through an index a site; so `without alloc` rejects every projection. A trait's method called through `Trait.m` names the impl's method the call reaches. The calculus has no key path, `for` over an `Iter`, `view.into` or `view.split`, so none of their sites or exceptions. The promise of `without alloc` counts the values that operations make, not the copies that a write through an index or a call for an in-place parameter makes of a shared list, which no step of the calculus's evaluators makes; the promises of `without copy` and of [facts.in-place-promise](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.in-place-promise) are left out ([Code without allocation or copies](#code-without-allocation-or-copies)).
- **[shared.locks](../reference/14-tasks.md#shared.locks)** is stated as the fact and E0430, not as a limit: `without locks` and its E0204 at each site, and what `stilt show` prints, are left out. A method `Trait.m` is a site when the function `m` of any impl of the trait has the fact, which may count a site that the checker, knowing which impl the use reaches, doesn't. The promise covers a `when`'s block run from a frame whose function values are lock-free: a call through a function value that code with the fact made may still take a cell ([Holding no value while waiting](#holding-no-value-while-waiting)). Of [facts.calls-through-function-values](../reference/15-facts.md#facts.calls-through-function-values), the model states what it says of `locks`, and of [shared.when-order](../reference/14-tasks.md#shared.when-order) that no block waits on a value while it holds one, in the sequential run.
- **Parts of paragraphs the theorem states** that the calculus leaves out, each paragraph's other rules in the theorem:
  - `exit(code)`, of [program.prelude-functions](../reference/04-programs.md#program.prelude-functions): the calculus has `panic`, `unreachable` and the writes to the output, and no operation that ends the program with a status.
  - Taking a value apart to build in its place, the last way of [values.consumed-parameters](../reference/05-values.md#values.consumed-parameters) to consume a parameter: the model's consumption is returning, storing, rebinding with `var` and passing to a consumed parameter.
  - An integer literal at a float type ([numbers.integer-literals](../reference/06-numbers.md#numbers.integer-literals)): `HasType.int` gives a literal an integer type only.
  - Compound assignment, `place op= e` ([expressions.assignment](../reference/08-expressions.md#expressions.assignment)), and so its order ([values.compound-assignment-order](../reference/05-values.md#values.compound-assignment-order)): the calculus has `place = e` alone.
  - Non-escaping parameters of a trait's methods ([traits.impl-signatures](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.impl-signatures)): a trait's signatures hold no row variable, so a function-typed parameter of a trait's method is `escaping`, and a call through a bound passes no argument's effects on.
  - A `resume` statement whose value isn't `()` ([handlers.resuming](../reference/12-effects.md#handlers.resuming)): `HasType.seq` asks a statement for `()`, so the model rejects `resume(v);` when `R` isn't `()`, which the Reference accepts.
  - The row of a task's function ([tasks.task-functions](../reference/14-tasks.md#tasks.task-functions)) is the hypothesis of the `tasks` promise, not a check: the export writes `s.spawn(f)` as a call of `f` through a binding, and the calculus has no `spawn` whose argument typing could check.
  - The float constants `NAN`, `INFINITY`, `PI` and `E` ([std.floats](../reference/17-std.md#std.floats)): a float literal is a decimal mantissa and exponent, so the calculus makes no NaN and no infinity from a literal.
  - The positions that must hold an irrefutable pattern, E0321 ([patterns.irrefutable-positions](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.irrefutable-positions)), and so the irrefutable pattern of `let P = (e else x)` ([patterns.else-after-pattern](../reference/09-patterns.md#patterns.else-after-pattern)): `Pattern.Irrefutable` defines irrefutability, which a let-else's pattern must lack (`HasType.letElse`), but a `let` or `var` with a pattern is a `match` with one arm, which typing asks only to be exhaustive, so `let A(x) | B(x) = e` passes; and the calculus has no arrays, whose list patterns are irrefutable when their elements are. The bare variant names of a let-else, which the Reference reads against the annotation or the type of `e`, are resolved by elaboration: each variant pattern names its type.
  - A format splice's default fill and alignment, by its written type ([std.width-fill-and-alignment](../reference/17-std.md#std.width-fill-and-alignment)): the export chooses them, and the calculus receives them chosen.
  - A trait's defaults and constants ([traits.functions-without-self](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.functions-without-self) and [traits.impl-members](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.impl-members)): a function of a trait has no default body in the calculus, so an impl holds a member for each, which for a default the export writes as the default's body with `Self` the impl's type and the trait's parameters the impl's arguments; and a trait declares no constant, so `T.ZERO` has no form, which the export writes as the expression of the value that the impl for the type gives, or of the trait's default, at that type: a constant expression, pure, which the calculus doesn't check for cycles (E0327, [traits.trait-constants](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.trait-constants)).
  - Member types and `where` clauses ([traits.member-types](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.member-types) and [traits.where](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.where)): elaboration declares a member type as a type parameter of its function (`Decls.MemberTypes`), so a `where` clause's bounds are those of that parameter, and writes a member type whose bound writes its argument, as `C: Collection<f64>`, as that argument, whose bounds in a `where` clause the model doesn't check (E0318 there). E0336, and a `where` clause of a trait impl, whose parameters' bounds write no trait arguments in the calculus, are left out. A trait's methods and its functions without `self` are named on a type alike, as `Trait.m` at it.
  - Impls of `Eq`, `Ord` and `Show` that the program writes ([traits.structural-impls](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.structural-impls) and [traits.unsatisfied-bounds](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.unsatisfied-bounds)): `Trait.m` names such an impl at its type, so `==`, the ordering operators and an interpolation at that type are calls of its methods (`MethodAt`), as the export writes them. But no bound is met through it: a bound of one of the three is met only through the built-in and structural impls, which a type with a written impl, and a type that holds one, don't have in the calculus, since an operation of a structural impl doesn't call the impls of the components. So the calculus states the structural impls that reach a written impl through a component only as the export writes them, as functions of its own at each type, composed of calls of the components' impls ([Executable semantics](#executable-semantics)). A type whose `Eq` the program writes is no map key, as [traits.hash-follows-written-eq](../reference/11-traits.md#traits.hash-follows-written-eq) says, nor is one whose `Eq` and `Hash` the program both writes, whose keys the calculus would find by those impls (`Decls.KeyStep`): the export writes a map of such keys as the list of its entries, with functions of its own that find a key by those impls. `View<T>`, which has `Eq`, `Ord` and `Show` where `T` has them ([views.functions-of-views](../reference/13-modes.md#views.functions-of-views)), implements none of them in the calculus either.
- **An effect in a row** is checked against the program's effects only in the types that declarations write: record fields, variant payloads, function signatures, operations and trait methods (`Decls.EffectsDeclared`, [handlers.effects-in-rows](../reference/12-effects.md#handlers.effects-in-rows)). A type written in a body, a closure literal's or a `let`'s, may name an effect that no declaration declares, which E0211 rejects. Such a row only widens what a function value may perform, so typing still asks a `with` for every effect that a body performs.
- **The value given to `Shared.new`** must be `portable` ([shared.std-sync](../reference/14-tasks.md#shared.std-sync)), which the model doesn't check: a view given to it is E0432. A type argument of a generic function's call may be a view type ([views.view-types](../reference/13-modes.md#views.view-types)), so the check would need what an instance knows of the modes of its type parameters' values.
- **The namespaces** of [declarations.namespaces](../reference/10-declarations.md#declarations.namespaces) are the program's, which has one module: the type namespace holds each record, variant type, trait and effect name once, and the functions are named once (`Decls.WellFormed`). A variant is named by its type and its own name, so two variant types may have variants of one name, which E0208 rejects in one module.
